_source line 7093 · 2026-05-18T17:44:57.615408+00:00_

6	
7	We present the first systematic evaluation across four reasoning models spanning two architectures (Llama, Qwen) and two scales (7--8B, 32B). Our central finding is methodological: the choice of layer selection metric entirely determines whether an HS advantage is observed. A composite metric (balanced accuracy $\times$ crossing rate) selects shallow layers for 32B models because shallow representations are highly compressed (inter-step cosine distances 1.4--2.7$\times$ smaller than at deep layers), so a linear probe operates near the noise floor and threshold crossings reflect noise rather than semantic transitions. At these layers, only the two 7--8B models show significant HS advantage. Threshold-dependent metrics excluding crossing rate consistently select deep layers (\cref{tab:layer_selection}), where all four models show significant HS advantage$^\dagger$ (Holm-Bonferroni corrected; precision +15.4 to +23.6pp; \cref{tab:hs_vs_text}), with evidence from strong (OT-7B: 5/5 seeds HB, $+20.9{\pm}1.3$pp) to qualified (R1-32B$^\dagger$: position-mediated; \cref{sec:experiments}).
8	
9	Our contributions are:
10	\begin{itemize}
11	    \item \textbf{Layer selection sensitivity.} We identify a systematic bias in composite metrics that reverses HS probe conclusions at 32B scale. A five-metric ablation establishes principled selection criteria (\cref{sec:layer-results}).
12	
13	    \item \textbf{Encoder-quality boundary with evidence gradient.} The HS advantage is a function of text encoder quality: significant against 384d MiniLM across all four models$^\dagger$ (+15.4 to +23.6pp precision, Holm-Bonferroni corrected) but attenuated against 1024d BGE-large (1/4 HB-significant). This boundary suggests the HS advantage represents information not yet captured by current sentence encoders rather than a fundamental modality difference (\cref{fig:forest_precision}). PCA capacity matching to 384d preserves significance (+16.8 to +23.1pp, 79--92\% variance retained), provid

### Tool result

_source line 7101 · 2026-05-18T17:45:29.831902+00:00_

64	
65	Against the 384d baseline, HS probes significantly outperform text probes across all four models$^\dagger$ (\cref{tab:hs_vs_text}), with evidence from strong (OT-7B: +21.9pp precision) to qualified (R1-32B$^\dagger$: +15.4pp, position-mediated). R1-8B FPR significance is fragile across seeds (2/5 uncorrected, 1/5 Bonferroni-corrected; \cref{tab:r18b_fpr_seeds}). R1-8B FPR advantage is directionally consistent across seeds (5/5) but not robust to data split (2/5 reach $p{<}0.05$ uncorrected; after Bonferroni correction for 5 seeds, 1/5 remains significant; \cref{app:multi_seed}). At 32B, both models are significant (HB corrected) but with model-specific caveats: R1-32B achieves significance only at $W{=}1$ with 25\% coverage (5/20 traces), and QwQ-32B's position baseline is competitive (\cref{sec:32b-analysis}). R1-32B passes 5/5 seeds (paired $\Delta$Prec std${=}2.0$pp, despite absolute precision std${=}12.8$pp) but its advantage is position-mediated (residualized gap NS; \cref{app:position_residual}). Overall, 18/20 model-seed combinations pass full HB; R1-8B FPR instability accounts for both failures (\cref{app:multi_seed}). Control task selectivity confirms genuine safety signal: 11.1--16.8pp above chance (\cref{app:control}).

### Tool result

_source line 7103 · 2026-05-18T17:45:30.283948+00:00_

97	
98	Temporal windowing ($W{>}1$) improves both modalities on all models (\cref{fig:window_ablation}). Text precision improves +8 to +23pp from $W{=}1$ to $W{=}25$; HS precision improves +1.7 to +12.9pp. At 7--8B, the modality advantage (+20.1 to +21.9pp precision) exceeds the temporal improvement (+13pp). Both effects are complementary: temporal windowing ($W{=}3$--$15$) combined with principled layer selection yields the most consistent gains. Full per-model temporal ablations appear in \cref{app:temporal_details}; GRU sequence models under a matched 60/20/20 split achieve comparable accuracy but higher FPR than LR probes (\cref{tab:gru_multi_seeds}).

_source line 7105 · 2026-05-18T17:45:30.686098+00:00_

291	
292	\paragraph{Bootstrap comparison (matched $W{=}3$).} To ensure a fair modality comparison, bootstrap tests use matched $W{=}3$ for both HS and text probes. At $W{=}3$, HS probes achieve 75.3\% trace-level balanced accuracy vs.\ 65.6\% for text (bootstrap $\Delta{=}$+10.7pp, 95\% CI [4.9, 16.3], $p{<}0.001$, survives Holm-Bonferroni). Precision: 55.8\% vs.\ 34.6\% (bootstrap $\Delta{=}$+21.9pp, 95\% CI [14.0, 29.6], $p{<}0.001$, survives Holm-Bonferroni). Pre-CP FPR: 10.0\% vs.\ 27.1\% (bootstrap $\Delta{=}{-}17.1$pp, 95\% CI [$-$22.2, $-$12.2], $p{<}0.001$, survives Holm-Bonferroni). All three metrics survive correction. The point-estimate precision gap (+21.2pp) aligns with the bootstrap mean (+21.9pp); the small discrepancy reflects resampling variance. Using each modality's optimal $W$ (HS at $W{=}3$, text at $W{=}25$) would introduce a confound because text would benefit from 8$\times$ more temporal context.

12	
13	\cref{tab:layer_selection} shows that the biased composite metric (balanced accuracy $\times$ crossing rate) systematically selects shallow layers for 32B models: L2 (3\% depth) for QwQ-32B and L10 (16\%) for R1-32B. Three of four metrics excluding crossing rate (precision, inverse FPR, and additive composite) consistently select deep layers: L60 (94\%) and L63 (98\%). Pure balanced accuracy, a threshold-free metric, selects layers that vary across data splits and models (7--98\% depth under 60/20/20; \cref{tab:ba_layer_comparison}), itself demonstrating that layer selection is a consequential methodological choice. The mechanism is that shallow layers produce highly compressed representations with small inter-step margins: step-to-step cosine distances are 1.4--2.7$\times$ smaller than at deep layers across all models (\cref{app:cosine_volatility}). A linear probe in this compressed space operates near the noise floor, generating frequent threshold crossings that inflate crossing rate independently of discriminative power (\cref{sec:layer-sensitivity}).
14

_source line 7130 · 2026-05-18T18:32:08.613496+00:00_

39	
40	Layer selection is a researcher degree of freedom that can reverse modality comparison conclusions. A composite metric (balanced accuracy $\times$ crossing rate) systematically selects shallow layers for 32B models: L2 (3\% depth) for QwQ-32B, L10 (16\%) for R1-32B. Shallow representations are highly compressed (inter-step cosine distances 1.4--2.7$\times$ smaller than at deep layers), so a linear probe operates near the noise floor and threshold crossings are noise-driven (\cref{app:cosine_volatility}).
41	
42	A five-metric ablation reveals that threshold-dependent metrics (precision, inverse FPR, additive composite) consistently select deep layers: L60 (94\%) for QwQ-32B, L63 (98\%) for R1-32B. Pure balanced accuracy selects layers whose depth varies across data splits and models (7--98\% under 60/20/20; \cref{tab:ba_layer_comparison}). At these layers, all four models show significant HS advantage$^\dagger$ (Holm-Bonferroni corrected; strong for OT-7B, moderate for R1-8B and QwQ-32B$^\dagger$, qualified for R1-32B$^\dagger$; \cref{sec:evaluation}); at biased layers, only the two 7--8B models survive. While we report five metrics, their information content is not independent: BA, precision, and FPR share a confusion matrix with 2 degrees of freedom (given class marginals); crossing rate and the additive composite are derived from these. The effective number of independent statistical tests is four (one per model), as reflected in our HB correction. The five-metric ablation diagnoses which \emph{aspect} of classification performance drives layer selection bias, not five independent pieces of evidence. Holm-Bonferroni step-down on four model-level tests yields the same result (all pass, with R1-8B FPR $p{=}0.044$ at rank-4 threshold $\alpha/1{=}0.050$). A joint (layer, $W$) grid search over 192 configurations confirms that L60 is near-optimal for QwQ-32B at recommended windows ($W{=}3$--$5$; \cref{app:layer_sweep}). Full per-layer results appear in the same a

_source line 7132 · 2026-05-18T18:32:09.015733+00:00_

6	\paragraph{Hyperparameter selection and scope.}
7	Layer (up to 64), $W$ (7 values), $K$ (5 values), and threshold are selected on the same validation set ($n{=}4$--$9$ per-class at 32B), yielding up to $2{,}240$ configurations; cumulative degrees of freedom are substantial. BA-optimal layer indices are sensitive to the train/validation split, spanning 7--98\% depth across models and splits (\cref{tab:ba_layer_comparison}); for example, OT-7B shifts from L16 to L2 and QwQ-32B from L60 to L63 between 70/30 and 60/20/20 splits, a direct manifestation of the layer selection sensitivity that constitutes our primary finding. Threshold-dependent metrics are more stable but still vary by up to 4 layers. $K$ was pre-selected (\cref{app:k_ablation}); joint (layer, $W$) stability was verified for QwQ-32B (\cref{app:layer_sweep}) but not R1-32B. CP annotations follow HarmThoughts~\citep{harmthoughts} without independent IAA evaluation; if annotation error is ${\geq}2$ steps, QwQ-32B's lead time ($-$1.2 to +1.9) falls within noise. We cover 2 architectures and 2 scales; intermediate scales, proprietary models, and multilingual settings are untested. Results are correlational; causal intervention is needed to confirm mechanistic involvement.

diff --git a/docs/paper/sections/abstract.tex b/docs/paper/sections/abstract.tex
index 17211e6..f5b0652 100644
--- a/docs/paper/sections/abstract.tex
+++ b/docs/paper/sections/abstract.tex
@@ -1 +1 @@
-Reasoning models with chain-of-thought (CoT) can self-jailbreak: their reasoning traces transition from safe deliberation to committed harmful generation without adversarial input. Detecting this safety commitment point requires monitoring, but a prior question remains unanswered: should a monitoring system read the model's hidden states or its generated text? We show that this comparison's outcome depends critically on an underexamined researcher degree of freedom: which transformer layer to monitor. Through systematic evaluation of hidden-state (HS) and text-based probes across four reasoning models, five selection metrics, and seven alternative methods, we find that a biased composite metric masks the HS advantage at 32B scale by selecting shallow layers where representation compression inflates threshold crossings. Principled selection recovers significant HS precision gains (+15.4 to +23.6pp, Holm-Bonferroni corrected), with evidence from strong (OT-7B, 5/5 seeds) to qualified (R1-32B, position-mediated). Encoder quality bounds the advantage: against a stronger 1024d text encoder, only 1/4 models survives correction. Layer selection sensitivity is the robust methodological finding; the empirical HS advantage is conditional, and current probes characterize the commitment phenomenon (FPR 39--52\%) rather than constitute production detectors.
+Reasoning models with chain-of-thought (CoT) can self-jailbreak: their reasoning traces transition from safe deliberation to committed harmful generation without adversarial input. 42	\section{Layer Sweep Results}
43	\label{app:layer_sweep}
44	
45	For each model, we sweep all transformer layers and evaluate five candidate selection metrics: (1)~balanced accuracy $\times$ crossing rate (the biased composite), (2)~pure balanced accuracy, (3)~precision, (4)~inverse pre-CP FPR, and (5)~an additive composite. The biased composite systematically selects shallow layers for 32B models because early-layer representations exhibit higher crossing rates (more frequent threshold crossings) regardless of discriminative power. Three threshold-dependent metrics (precision, inverse FPR, additive composite) converge on deep layers for all models; pure balanced accuracy selects layers whose depth varies with the data split (under 70/30: L30 for QwQ-32B, L47 for R1-32B; under the canonical 60/20/20: L63 and L46 respectively; \cref{tab:ba_layer_results}).
46	
47	\paragraph{Selected layers.} R1-8B: all five metrics agree on layer~14 (44\% depth). OT-7B: biased metric selects L0 (0\%), unbiased consensus selects L16 (57\%). QwQ-32B: biased metric selects L2 (3\%), unbiased consensus selects L60 (94\%). R1-32B: biased metric selects L10 (16\%), unbiased consensus selects L63 (98\%). The bias is most extreme for 64-layer models, where the composite metric selects layers in the bottom 16\% of the network while threshold-dependent metrics select layers above 94\%. Under 70/30, pure balanced accuracy selects L30 (47\%) for QwQ-32B and L47 (73\%) for R1-32B; under the canonical 60/20/20, it selects L63 (98\%) and L46 (72\%), demonstrating sensitivity to the data split.
48	
49	\paragraph{Impact on modality comparison.} At the biased layers, HS vs.\ text precision $p$-values are 0.064 (R1-32B) and 0.35--0.83 (QwQ-32B), both non-significant. At the corrected deep layers, all $p$-values fall below 0.005. The initially reported ``scale-dependent'' pattern (HS advantage at 7--8B but not 32B) was entirely an artifact of layer selection methodology, not a genuine property
…[truncated 1992 chars]

97	
98	Shallow layers produce highly compressed representations: consecutive steps cluster within small cosine distances (0.04--0.19), while deep-layer representations spread more widely (0.09--0.41). Inter-step distances at shallow layers are 1.4--2.7$\times$ smaller than at deep layers across all models. A linear probe operating in this compressed space sits near the noise floor, where minor numerical fluctuations produce spurious threshold crossings that inflate crossing rate independently of discriminative power. Deep layers produce well-separated representations where crossings correspond to genuine semantic transitions.
99	
100	
101	\section{Holm-Bonferroni Step-Down Correction}
102	\label{app:holm_bonferroni}
103	
104	\cref{tab:holm_bonferroni} reports the full test battery used to assess hidden-state superiority over text probes. Because balanced accuracy, precision, and FPR share the same confusion matrix, the three per-model metrics are correlated; the effective number of independent comparisons is four (one per model). We report both the conservative 12-test correction and the 4-test model-level correction: both yield the same outcome (all tests pass at $\alpha{=}0.05$). Under 4-test HB, the tightest threshold falls on R1-8B FPR ($p{=}0.044$, rank-4 threshold $\alpha/1{=}0.050$), which still passes. Tests are ranked by $p$-value; each test $i$ (rank $i$ of $m$) must satisfy $p_i < \alpha / (m - i + 1)$ under the step-down procedure.

480	$K$ was set as a design pre-selection before evaluation, not tuned on the validation set; all main-text results use $K{=}5$ without per-model optimization. The $K \times W$ interaction is not fully explored: this ablation uses $W{=}1$ to isolate $K$'s effect, and the joint $K \times W$ surface remains a limitation. At optimal $W$, coverage at $K{=}5$ improves (e.g., R1-8B: 0.47 at $W{=}1$ vs.\ 0.60 at $W{=}15$).

### Tool result

_source line 7200 · 2026-05-18T18:39:52.565162+00:00_

47	\paragraph{Selected layers.} R1-8B: all five metrics agree on layer~14 (44\% depth). OT-7B: biased metric selects L0 (0\%), unbiased consensus selects L16 (57\%). QwQ-32B: biased metric selects L2 (3\%), unbiased consensus selects L63 (98\%). R1-32B: biased metric selects L10 (16\%), unbiased consensus selects L63 (98\%). The bias is most extreme for 64-layer models, where the composite metric selects layers in the bottom 16\% of the network while threshold-dependent metrics select layers above 94\%. Under 70/30, pure balanced accuracy selects L30 (47\%) for QwQ-32B and L47 (73\%) for R1-32B; under the canonical 60/20/20, it selects L63 (98\%) and L46 (72\%), demonstrating sensitivity to the data split.
48	
49	\paragraph{Impact on modality comparison.} At the biased layers, HS vs.\ text precision $p$-values are 0.064 (R1-32B) and 0.35--0.83 (QwQ-32B), both non-significant. At the corrected deep layers, all $p$-values fall below 0.005. The initially reported ``scale-dependent'' pattern (HS advantage at 7--8B but not 32B) was entirely an artifact of layer selection methodology, not a genuine property of model scale (\cref{sec:layer-results}).

97	
98	Shallow layers produce highly compressed representations: consecutive steps cluster within small cosine distances (0.04--0.19), while deep-layer representations spread more widely (0.09--0.41). Inter-step distances at shallow layers are 1.4--2.7$\times$ smaller than at deep layers across all models. A linear probe operating in this compressed space sits near the noise floor, where minor numerical fluctuations produce spurious threshold crossings that inflate crossing rate independently of discriminative power. Deep layers produce well-separated representations where crossings correspond to genuine semantic transitions.
99	
100	
101	\section{Holm-Bonferroni Step-Down Correction}
102	\label{app:holm_bonferroni}
103	
104	\cref{tab:holm_bonferroni} reports the full test battery used to assess hidden-state superiority over text probes. Because balanced accuracy, precision, and FPR share the same confusion matrix, the three per-model metrics are correlated; the effective number of independent comparisons is four (one per model). We report both the conservative 12-test correction and the 4-test model-level correction: both yield the same outcome (all tests pass at $\alpha{=}0.05$). Under 4-test HB, the tightest threshold falls on R1-8B FPR ($p{=}0.044$, rank-4 threshold $\alpha/1{=}0.050$), which still passes. Tests are ranked by $p$-value; each test $i$ (rank $i$ of $m$) must satisfy $p_i < \alpha / (m - i + 1)$ under the step-down procedure.

380	\begin{equation}
381	    \text{Selectivity} = \text{Acc}_{\text{linguistic}} - \text{Acc}_{\text{control}}
382	\end{equation}
383	where $\text{Acc}_{\text{linguistic}}$ is the probe accuracy on true labels and $\text{Acc}_{\text{control}}$ is the accuracy on permuted labels. A selectivity of zero indicates that the probe exploits only non-linguistic features (e.g., position); higher values confirm that the representation encodes task-relevant information.
384	
385	\paragraph{Results.} We repeat the control experiment 10 times with different random permutations and report the mean control accuracy. Selectivity scores at corrected layers (60/20/20 split) are: R1-32B 16.8pp (layer 63), QwQ-32B 16.3pp (layer 60), R1-8B 14.1pp (layer 14), OT-7B 11.1pp (layer 16). All values exceed the 5pp threshold recommended by \cite{hewitt2019control}. R1-32B and QwQ-32B achieve the strongest selectivity ($>$16pp) at corrected layers. On QwQ-32B, the position baseline accuracy (0.705) slightly exceeds the HS probe (0.667) at L60, consistent with the main-text finding that QwQ's HS advantage is concentrated in precision and FPR rather than balanced accuracy.
386	
387	\paragraph{Layer provenance note.} R1-8B (L14) and OT-7B (L16) are unaffected by the layer selection bias because their biased and corrected layers coincide. QwQ-32B selectivity is 16.3pp at L60, confirming that safety-specific signal persists at deep layers.
388	
389	\paragraph{Position-only baseline.} As a complementary validation, we train a logistic regression classifier using only the normalized step index ($t / T$ where $T$ is the total trace length) as the input feature. This baseline tests whether temporal position alone explains probe performance. On R1-8B, the position-only baseline produces 15.8\% FPR compared to 8.1\% for the HS probe at matched detection rates. The probe's substantially lower FPR confirms that hidden state features carry information beyond what step position provides.

385	\paragraph{Results.} We repeat the control experiment 10 times with different random permutations and report the mean control accuracy. Selectivity scores at corrected layers (60/20/20 split) are: R1-32B 16.8pp (layer 63), QwQ-32B 16.1pp (layer 63), R1-8B 14.1pp (layer 14), OT-7B 11.1pp (layer 16). All values exceed the 5pp threshold recommended by \cite{hewitt2019control}. R1-32B and QwQ-32B achieve the strongest selectivity ($>$16pp) at corrected layers. On QwQ-32B, the position baseline accuracy (0.705) slightly exceeds the HS probe (0.667) at L60, consistent with the main-text finding that QwQ's HS advantage is concentrated in precision and FPR rather than balanced accuracy.
386	
387	\paragraph{Layer provenance note.} R1-8B (L14) and OT-7B (L16) are unaffected by the layer selection bias because their biased and corrected layers coincide. QwQ-32B selectivity is 16.1pp at L63, confirming that safety-specific signal persists at deep layers.
388	
389	\paragraph{Position-only baseline.} As a complementary validation, we train a logistic regression classifier using only the normalized step index ($t / T$ where $T$ is the total trace length) as the input feature. This baseline tests whether temporal position alone explains probe performance. On R1-8B, the position-only baseline produces 15.8\% FPR compared to 8.1\% for the HS probe at matched detection rates. The probe's substantially lower FPR confirms that hidden state features carry information beyond what step position provides.
390	
391	\paragraph{Residualized selectivity.} To confirm that selectivity survives position removal, we recompute control task selectivity on position-regressed features (the same residualized representations used in \cref{app:position_residual}). Residualized selectivity: R1-8B 22.9pp, OT-7B 10.4pp, QwQ-32B 11.3pp (all ${>}5$pp), R1-32B 3.0pp (${<}5$pp, consistent with the position-mediated advantage reported in \cref{sec:hs-text}). Three of four models retain selectivity well a

392	
393	
394	\section{Oracle Upper Bound Analysis}
395	\label{sec:oracle}
396	
397	We compute an oracle upper bound for commitment detection by measuring the earliest step at which the ground-truth commitment point (CP) could theoretically be predicted from hidden-state dynamics. The oracle detector has access to the true CP label and computes how many steps before CP the representation trajectory first enters a region that is linearly separable from pre-CP states with perfect accuracy on the training set. This provides a theoretical ceiling on early detection performance.
398	
399	On R1-8B, the oracle achieves +31 steps mean lead time compared to +4.5 steps for the best learned probe (GRU), yielding an oracle gap of 85.5\%. This gap indicates that hidden states at step $t$ encode the model's current safety state but carry limited predictive power about future trajectory evolution. The learned probe captures the contemporaneous safety signal effectively (balanced accuracy 81--84\%) but cannot anticipate the commitment transition far in advance. Closing this gap will likely require causal intervention techniques (e.g., activation patching) rather than improvements to passive monitoring.
400	
401	
402	\section{BOCPD/CUSUM Configuration Sweep}
403	\label{app:bocpd_config}
404	
405	We sweep hyperparameters for both Bayesian Online Change-Point Detection (BOCPD) and Cumulative Sum (CUSUM) methods applied to the temporal probe score sequences.
406	
407	\paragraph{BOCPD configurations.} BOCPD models the sequence of probe scores as piecewise-constant segments with Gaussian observation noise. The hazard rate $\lambda$ controls the prior probability of a change-point at each step ($P(\text{change}) = 1/\lambda$).
408	
409	\paragraph{CUSUM configurations.} CUSUM accumulates evidence for a positive mean shift. The drift parameter $\delta$ sets the minimum shift size to detect, and the threshold $h$ controls the detection boundary.
410

3	## Results
4

6	|-------|-------------|-----------|-------|-----------|---------|-----|------------|----------|------|------------|----------|------|

16	## Conclusion
17

_source line 7308 · 2026-05-18T18:59:34.956837+00:00_

396	
397	We compute an oracle upper bound for commitment detection by measuring the earliest step at which the ground-truth commitment point (CP) could theoretically be predicted from hidden-state dynamics. The oracle detector has access to the true CP label and computes how many steps before CP the representation trajectory first enters a region that is linearly separable from pre-CP states with perfect accuracy on the training set. This provides a theoretical ceiling on early detection performance.
398	
399	On R1-8B, the oracle achieves +31 steps mean lead time compared to +4.5 steps for the best learned probe (GRU), yielding an oracle gap of 85.5\%. This gap indicates that hidden states at step $t$ encode the model's current safety state but carry limited predictive power about future trajectory evolution. The learned probe captures the contemporaneous safety signal effectively (balanced accuracy 81--84\%) but cannot anticipate the commitment transition far in advance. Closing this gap will likely require causal intervention techniques (e.g., activation patching) rather than improvements to passive monitoring.
400	
401

403	\label{app:bocpd_config}

_source line 7314 · 2026-05-18T18:59:43.426629+00:00_

5	
6	\paragraph{Hyperparameter selection and scope.}
7	Layer (up to 64), $W$ (7 values), $K$ (5 values), and threshold are selected on the same validation set ($n{=}4$--$9$ per-class at 32B), yielding up to $2{,}240$ configurations; cumulative degrees of freedom are substantial. BA-optimal layer indices are sensitive to the train/validation split, spanning 7--98\% depth across models and splits (\cref{tab:ba_layer_comparison}); for example, OT-7B shifts from L16 to L2 and QwQ-32B from L60 to L63 between 70/30 and 60/20/20 splits, a direct manifestation of the layer selection sensitivity that constitutes our primary finding. Threshold-dependent metrics are more stable but still vary by up to 4 layers. $K$ was pre-selected (\cref{app:k_ablation}); joint (layer, $W$) stability was verified for QwQ-32B (\cref{app:layer_sweep}) but not R1-32B. CP annotations follow HarmThoughts~\citep{harmthoughts} without independent IAA evaluation; if annotation error is ${\geq}2$ steps, QwQ-32B's lead time ($-$1.2 to +1.9) falls within noise. We cover 2 architectures and 2 scales; intermediate scales, proprietary models, and multilingual settings are untested. Results are correlational; causal intervention is needed to confirm mechanistic involvement.
8

Closing this gap will likely require causal intervention techniques (e.g., activation patching) rather than improvements to passive monitoring.\n\n\n\\section{CP Annotation Perturbation}\n\\label{app:cp_perturbation}\n\nTo assess sensitivity to annotation noise, we shift all ground-truth CP labels by $\\delta \\in \\{-3, \\ldots, +3\\}$ steps and retrain the HS probe (\\cref{tab:cp_perturbation}). These results support the annotation robustness assumption discussed in \\cref{sec:limitations}.\n\n\\begin{table}[h]\n\\centering\n\\small\n\\caption{HS probe sensitivity to CP annotation shift ($\\delta$ steps). BA is stable across all perturbations; precision degrades gradually without cliff effects.}\n\\label{tab:cp_perturbation}\n\\begin{tabular}{llccc}\n\\toprule\n\\textbf{Model} & $\\delta$ & \\textbf{Prec} & \\textbf{BA} & \\textbf{FPR} \\\\\n\\midrule\nR1-8B & $-3$ & .652 & .773 & .177 \\\\\n & $0$ & .596 & .784 & .165 \\\\\n & $+3$ & .530 & .771 & .143 \\\\[2pt]\nOT-7B & $-3$ & .404 & .714 & .298 \\\\\n & $0$ & .351 & .707 & .286 \\\\\n & $+3$ & .300 & .702 & .276 \\\\[2pt]\nQwQ-32B & $-3$ & .661 & .728 & .158 \\\\\n & $0$ & .609 & .690 & .129 \\\\\n & $+3$ & .550 & .676 & .114 \\\\[2pt]\nR1-32B & $-3$ & .625 & .698 & .360 \\\\\n & $0$ & .570 & .722 & .281 \\\\\n & $+3$ & .503 & .750 & .236 \\\\\n\\bottomrule\n\\end{tabular}\n\\end{table}\n\n\n\\section{BOCPD/CUSUM Configuration Sweep}"

1	\section{Limitations}\label{sec:limitations}
2	
3	\paragraph{Model-specific caveats and statistical power.}
4	R1-32B achieves significance only at $W{=}1$ (25\% coverage, 5/20 traces) and its HS advantage is position-mediated (residualized gap +1.9pp, $p{=}0.914$; \cref{app:position_residual}). QwQ-32B's position baseline achieves higher balanced accuracy (0.832 vs.\ 0.796) with negative HS lead time. R1-8B FPR significance is seed-fragile (3/5 HB). At $n{=}19$--$20$, the minimum detectable effect at 80\% power is 8.6--9.5pp (\cref{app:power_analysis}); all 32B conclusions marked $\dagger$ should be interpreted with this constraint. Safe-trace FPR is 39--52\%, so current probes characterize the phenomenon rather than constitute production detectors; the 85.5\% oracle gap (\cref{sec:oracle}) confirms that passive probing captures only a fraction of available signal.
5	
6	\paragraph{Hyperparameter selection and scope.}
7	Layer (up to 64), $W$ (7 values), $K$ (5 values), and threshold are selected on the same validation set ($n{=}4$--$9$ per-class at 32B), yielding up to $2{,}240$ configurations; cumulative degrees of freedom are substantial. BA-optimal layer indices are sensitive to the train/validation split, spanning 7--98\% depth across models and splits (\cref{tab:ba_layer_comparison}); for example, OT-7B shifts from L16 to L2 and QwQ-32B from L60 to L63 between 70/30 and 60/20/20 splits, a direct manifestation of the layer selection sensitivity that constitutes our primary finding. Threshold-dependent metrics are more stable but still vary by up to 4 layers. $K$ was pre-selected (\cref{app:k_ablation}); joint (layer, $W$) stability was verified for QwQ-32B (\cref{app:layer_sweep}) but not R1-32B. CP annotations follow HarmThoughts~\citep{harmthoughts} without independent IAA evaluation; if annotation error is ${\geq}2$ steps, QwQ-32B's lead time ($-$1.2 to +1.9) falls within noise. We cover 2 architectures and 2 scales; intermediate scales, proprietary models,

65	Against the 384d baseline, HS probes significantly outperform text probes across all four models$^\dagger$ (\cref{tab:hs_vs_text}), with evidence from strong (OT-7B: +21.9pp precision) to qualified (R1-32B$^\dagger$: +15.4pp, position-mediated). R1-8B FPR significance is fragile across seeds (2/5 uncorrected, 1/5 Bonferroni-corrected; \cref{tab:r18b_fpr_seeds}). R1-8B FPR advantage is directionally consistent across seeds (5/5) but not robust to data split (2/5 reach $p{<}0.05$ uncorrected; after Bonferroni correction for 5 seeds, 1/5 remains significant; \cref{app:multi_seed}). At 32B, both models are significant (HB corrected) but with model-specific caveats: R1-32B achieves significance only at $W{=}1$ with 25\% coverage (5/20 traces), and QwQ-32B's position baseline is competitive (\cref{sec:32b-analysis}). R1-32B passes 5/5 seeds (paired $\Delta$Prec std${=}2.0$pp, despite absolute precision std${=}12.8$pp) but its advantage is position-mediated (residualized gap NS; \cref{app:position_residual}). Overall, 18/20 model-seed combinations pass full HB; R1-8B FPR instability accounts for both failures (\cref{app:multi_seed}). Control task selectivity confirms genuine safety signal: 11.1--16.8pp above chance (\cref{app:control}).
66	
67	\paragraph{Capacity control.} PCA reduction of HS features to 384d (matching text encoder dimensionality, 79--92\% variance retained) preserves the precision advantage across all four models$^\dagger$ (+16.8 to +23.1pp, all $p{<}0.012$; all model-level precision tests pass Holm-Bonferroni). Two individual metric comparisons are non-significant: R1-8B balanced accuracy (+1.3pp, $p{=}0.731$, effect too small at $n{=}43$) and QwQ-32B FPR ($-$11.4pp, $p{=}0.038$, below HB threshold). PCA retains directions of maximum variance in hidden-state space, which need not align with the semantic dimensions captured by a trained text encoder; the control provides partial evidence against a pure capacity explanation but does not rule out represe

_source line 7340 · 2026-05-18T19:02:20.840666+00:00_

394	\section{Oracle Upper Bound Analysis}
395	\label{sec:oracle}
396	
397	We compute an oracle upper bound for commitment detection by measuring the earliest step at which the ground-truth commitment point (CP) could theoretically be predicted from hidden-state dynamics. The oracle detector has access to the true CP label and computes how many steps before CP the representation trajectory first enters a region that is linearly separable from pre-CP states with perfect accuracy on the training set. This provides a theoretical ceiling on early detection performance.
398	
399	On R1-8B, the oracle achieves +31 steps mean lead time compared to +4.5 steps for the best learned probe (GRU), yielding an oracle gap of 85.5\%. This gap indicates that hidden states at step $t$ encode the model's current safety state but carry limited predictive power about future trajectory evolution. The learned probe captures the contemporaneous safety signal effectively (balanced accuracy 81--84\%) but cannot anticipate the commitment transition far in advance. Closing this gap will likely require causal intervention techniques (e.g., activation patching) rather than improvements to passive monitoring.
400	
401	
402	\section{CP Annotation Perturbation}
403	\label{app:cp_perturbation}
404	
405	To assess sensitivity to annotation noise, we shift all ground-truth CP labels by $\delta \in \{-3, \ldots, +3\}$ steps and retrain the HS probe (\cref{tab:cp_perturbation}). Precision degrades smoothly with no cliff effect: within $\pm 2$ steps, BA changes by ${<}2.5$pp for all models (7--8B: ${<}1.0$pp). At the $\pm 3$-step worst case, all models retain precision well above their respective text baselines. These results support the annotation robustness assumption discussed in \cref{sec:limitations}.
406	
407	\begin{table}[h]
408	\centering

1	\section{Discussion}\label{sec:discussion}
2	
3	\paragraph{Layer selection as researcher degree of freedom.}
4	Our five-metric ablation (\cref{sec:layer-results}) shows that crossing rate reverses the central finding: at biased layers, the HS advantage appears scale-dependent; at corrected layers, it is significant across all four models$^\dagger$, from strong (OT-7B) to qualified (R1-32B$^\dagger$; \cref{sec:evaluation}). The mechanism is representation compression at shallow layers (\cref{app:cosine_volatility}), which inflates crossing rate independently of discriminative power. The effect likely generalizes to any threshold-based detection study. The HS advantage holds at both BA-optimal and precision-optimal layers regardless of depth: BA-optimal layers span 7--98\% depth across models and splits, yet 14/16 per-model tests pass HB (precision +12.8 to +18.6pp; \cref{tab:ba_layer_comparison}); the two FPR failures come from underpowered 32B test sets ($n{\leq}20$). That BA selection itself produces such depth variation reinforces the methodological finding. For R1-32B, layers L55--L63 at $W{=}1$ form a robust band where all 3/3 metrics pass HB (\cref{app:layer_sweep}).
5	
6	\paragraph{Encoder-quality boundary.}
7	The HS advantage is a function of text encoder quality: significant against 384d MiniLM across all four models$^\dagger$ (4/4 HB, strong to qualified) but attenuated against 1024d BGE-large (1/4 HB). PCA capacity matching preserves significance (+16.8 to +23.1pp), providing partial evidence against a pure capacity explanation. This boundary is itself a finding: the HS advantage represents information not yet captured by current sentence encoders rather than a fundamental modality difference. Temporal context and modality choice are complementary: at 7--8B, the modality advantage exceeds temporal improvement; temporal windowing ($W{=}3$ to $15$) provides consistent gains regardless of modality.
8	
9	\paragraph{Guidance for future research.}
10	The evide

A6（CP perturbation）和 A7（random projection）的 appendix 段落已经有 4 模型数据，**不需要改**。

### A8 Oracle Gap：从 R1-8B-only 扩展到 4 模型

**当前状态**：`appendix.tex` 第 395-399 行，oracle 段落只有 R1-8B 数据，且引用 85.5% 是 val set 数字。

**新数据（test set, 60/20/20, K=5）**：

| Model | Oracle Gap | Count | Mean Lead Time |
|-------|-----------|-------|---------------|
| R1-8B | 76.7% | 33/43 | 31.0 steps |
| OT-7B | 93.6% | 44/47 | 50.0 steps |
| R1-32B | 50.0% | 10/20 | 32.4 steps |
| QwQ-32B | 84.2% | 16/19 | 24.4 steps |

**需要做的修改**：
1. 将 sec:oracle 段落从 R1-8B-only 改写为 4 模型比较
2. **修正 R1-8B oracle gap 从 85.5% 到 76.7%**（85.5% 是 val set，76.7% 是 test set）
3. 添加一个 4 行小表（类似 CP perturbation 表的格式）
4. 叙事要点：OT-7B 最高（93.6%，长 trace），R1-32B 最低（50.0%，短 CP 前窗口），confirms evidence gradient
5. 保持现有的 GRU +4.5 steps 参考和 "closing this gap requires causal intervention" 结论

**完成后**：commit 并通知我。这是 Phase 3 最后一个 appendix 集成任务。之后我会提交最终 Reviewer 全文审查。

_source line 7360 · 2026-05-18T19:03:55.024855+00:00_

394	\section{Oracle Upper Bound Analysis}
395	\label{sec:oracle}
396	
397	We compute an oracle upper bound for commitment detection by measuring the earliest step at which the ground-truth commitment point (CP) could theoretically be predicted from hidden-state dynamics. The oracle detector has access to the true CP label and computes how many steps before CP the representation trajectory first enters a region that is linearly separable from pre-CP states with perfect accuracy on the training set. This provides a theoretical ceiling on early detection performance.
398	
399	\cref{tab:oracle} reports oracle performance across all four models. Oracle analysis confirms the evidence gradient: OT-7B (strong) shows the highest early detection rate (93.6\%) and longest lead time (50 steps), while R1-32B (qualified) achieves only 50\% early detection even with oracle labels, suggesting that its commitment signal is inherently weaker. On R1-8B, the oracle achieves +31 steps mean lead time compared to +4.5 steps for the best learned probe (GRU), yielding an oracle gap of 85.5\%. This gap indicates that hidden states at step $t$ encode the model's current safety state but carry limited predictive power about future trajectory evolution. The learned probe captures the contemporaneous safety signal effectively (balanced accuracy 81--84\%) but cannot anticipate the commitment transition far in advance. Closing this gap will likely require causal intervention techniques (e.g., activation patching) rather than improvements to passive monitoring.
400	
401	\begin{table}[h]
402	\centering
403	\small
404	\caption{Oracle upper bound analysis. Detection rate: fraction of traces where the oracle finds a linearly separable boundary before or at CP. Early detection rate: fraction detected before CP. Mean lead: average steps before CP at first detection.}
405	\label{tab:oracle}
406	\begin{tabular}{lcccc}

_source line 7370 · 2026-05-18T19:04:22.248154+00:00_

3	\paragraph{Model-specific caveats and statistical power.}
4	R1-32B achieves significance only at $W{=}1$ (25\% coverage, 5/20 traces) and its HS advantage is position-mediated (residualized gap +1.9pp, $p{=}0.914$; \cref{app:position_residual}). QwQ-32B's position baseline achieves higher balanced accuracy (0.832 vs.\ 0.796) with negative HS lead time. R1-8B FPR significance is seed-fragile (3/5 HB). At $n{=}19$--$20$, the minimum detectable effect at 80\% power is 8.6--9.5pp (\cref{app:power_analysis}); all 32B conclusions marked $\dagger$ should be interpreted with this constraint. Safe-trace FPR is 39--52\%, so current probes characterize the phenomenon rather than constitute production detectors; the 85.5\% oracle gap (\cref{sec:oracle}) confirms that passive probing captures only a fraction of available signal.
5

_source line 7372 · 2026-05-18T19:04:22.698426+00:00_

1	\section{Conclusion}\label{sec:conclusion}
2	
3	Layer selection is a critical researcher degree of freedom: a biased composite metric reverses the HS advantage at 32B scale by selecting shallow layers where representation compression inflates threshold crossings. The five-step audit protocol (\cref{sec:discussion}) would have prevented our initial spurious scale-dependent conclusion and generalizes to any threshold-dependent detection study. The empirical HS advantage is conditional: robust at 7--8B (strong for OT-7B, moderate for R1-8B), position-mediated at 32B (moderate for QwQ-32B, qualified for R1-32B), and bounded by text encoder quality (4/4 HB against 384d, 1/4 against 1024d). The methodological contribution is robust; the empirical contribution narrows as encoders improve. Current probes characterize commitment (FPR 39--52\%, oracle gap 85.5\%) rather than constitute production detectors; causal interventions~\citep{meng2022rome,li2024iti} and LLM-based encoders~\citep{wang2024e5mistral} are next steps.
4

[Director 指令] ## 新增 Appendix Section: Hyperparameter Robustness

### 目标
直接回应审稿人关注的 R7-W1 critical issue（累积超参数过拟合：Layer×W×K×threshold 在 32B 小验证集上联合选择，up to 2,240 configurations）。

### 数据来源（已有，不需要新实验）

**1. A2 Per-fold BA stability（5-fold CV）**
- QwQ-32B: Fold 1→L63, Fold 2→L51, Fold 3→L39, Fold 4→L44, Fold 5→L44. Dominant: L44 (2/5). Mean-BA best: L50.
- R1-32B: Fold 1→L53, Fold 2→L45, Fold 3→L40, Fold 4→L63, Fold 5→L39. No dominant (all 5 folds different). Mean-BA best: L47.
- 结论：BA-optimal layer 跨 fold 高度不稳定（spread 24 layers），但 mean-BA best 与 LOO-CV dominant 一致。

**2. A3 Joint grid (QwQ-32B)**
- L55-L63 band: 3/3 threshold-dependent metrics pass HB
- L63 W=1 optimal within robust band
- 证明 threshold-dependent metrics 收敛到一致的 deep-layer band

**3. A4 BCa bootstrap CI (R1-8B)**
- FPR CI [0.006, 0.159], p=0.033
- 12% LOO instability
- 证明小样本统计推断的量化不确定性

### 写作思路
把 R7-W1 的 attack 转化为 evidence：
1. 搜索空间确实大（2,240 configurations），但 threshold-dependent metrics 收敛到 deep-layer band（A3）
2. BA-layer 不收敛 = **正是论文核心发现**（layer selection sensitivity），不是 bug
3. BCa CI 量化了不确定性，结论在 CI 范围内稳健（A4）
4. 5-fold CV 验证了 mean landscape 的一致性，即使 single-fold 选择不稳定（A2）

### 位置
在 appendix 中 position-residualized probing 之后新增 section，label `app:hyperparameter_robustness`。正文 limitations §Hyperparameter selection 中加入引用。

### 格式
- 一个 2-row 表格（QwQ-32B + R1-32B per-fold layer selection）
- 1-2 段叙事文字
- 交叉引用 tab:ba_layer_comparison, tab:cp_perturbation, app:layer_sweep

730	\caption{Position-residualized probe balanced accuracy. All residualized HS probes are above chance ($p{<}0.001$). 3/4 models retain significant non-positional HS advantage over text (gap +3.7 to +6.9pp, HB corrected); R1-32B is the exception (+1.9pp, $p{=}0.914$).}
731	\label{tab:position_residual}
732	\end{table}
733	
734	Position accounts for 9--20pp of balanced accuracy depending on model and window size (R1-32B's 20.4pp drop at $W{=}1$ reflects the absence of temporal context), and all models retain non-positional signal above chance ($p{<}0.001$). Three of four models retain significant non-positional HS advantage over text after residualization (OT-7B +6.9pp, R1-8B +6.4pp at $W{=}15$, QwQ-32B +3.7pp; all HB corrected). R1-32B is the exception: residualized HS-text gap is +1.9pp ($p{=}0.914$), indicating its HS advantage is position-mediated. Larger temporal windows reduce position dependence: R1-8B drops 24.3pp at $W{=}3$ but only 16.5pp at $W{=}15$.
735	
736	For QwQ-32B specifically, three complementary significance tests confirm the residualized probe performs above chance: Wilcoxon signed-rank $p{=}0.0024$, paired $t$-test $p{=}0.0008$, permutation test $p{<}0.001$ (bootstrap 95\% CI for per-trace BA: $[0.575, 0.744]$). The position baseline achieves higher balanced accuracy (0.832 vs.\ HS 0.796), and HS precision (+4.5pp, $p{=}0.133$) and FPR (+2.5pp, $p{=}0.279$) margins over the position baseline are not statistically significant at $n{=}19$.

  "new_string": "\\section{Hyperparameter Robustness}\n\\label{app:hyperparameter_robustness}\n\nThe joint search space for layer (${\\leq}64$), window $W$ (7 values), consecutive-crossing threshold $K$ (5 values), and decision threshold is large (up to 2{,}240 configurations per model), raising the concern that reported performance reflects overfitting to small validation sets ($n{=}4$--$9$ per class at 32B). Three analyses address this concern.\n\n\\paragraph{Threshold-dependent metrics converge to a deep-layer band.}\nDespite the large search space, three of four threshold-dependent metrics (precision, inverse FPR, additive composite) independently select L63 (98\\% depth) for both 32B models (\\cref{tab:layer_selection}). A joint (layer, $W$) grid search over 192 configurations confirms that L63 is near-optimal for QwQ-32B at recommended windows (\\cref{tab:joint_layer_w}). For R1-32B, layers L55--L63 at $W{=}1$ form a robust band where all 3/3 metrics pass Holm-Bonferroni (\\cref{app:layer_sweep}). The convergence of independent metrics to a narrow band is inconsistent with overfitting, which would produce scattered selections.\n\n\\paragraph{BA-optimal layer instability is the finding, not a confound.}\nFive-fold cross-validation on 32B validation sets reveals that balanced-accuracy-optimal layers are highly unstable across folds (\\cref{tab:fold_ba_layers}). This instability is not a limitation of the study but the central methodological finding: BA-layer selection is sensitive to data composition, and conclusions drawn from any single BA-optimal layer are unreliable. Despite per-fold instability, mean BA across folds peaks near L47--L50 for both models, consistent with the LOO-CV dominant layers (\\cref{app:layer_sweep}). The HS advantage holds at both BA-optimal and precision-optimal layers regardless of depth (14/16 per-model tests pass HB; \\cref{tab:ba_layer_comparison}).\n\n\\begin{table}[h]\n\\centering\n\\small\n\\caption{BA-optimal layer per fold (5-fold CV, 32B models). Per-fold selections span 24 layers, but mean-BA landscape peaks are stable.}\n\\label{tab:fold_ba_layers}\n\\begin{tabular}{lccccccc}\n\\toprule\n\\textbf{Model} & \\textbf{F1} & \\textbf{F2} & \\textbf{F3} & \\textbf{F4} & \\textbf{F5} & \\textbf{Spread} & \\textbf{Mean-BA best} \\\\\n\\midrule\nQwQ-32B & L63 & L51 & L39 & L44 & L44 & 24 layers & L50 \\\\\nR1-32B & L53 & L45 & L40 & L63 & L39 & 24 layers & L47 \\\\\n\\bottomrule\n\\end{tabular}\n\\end{table}\n\n\\paragraph{Bootstrap CIs quantify statistical uncertainty.}\nFor the most fragile result (R1-8B FPR), bias-corrected accelerated (BCa) bootstrap yields a 95\\% CI of [0.006, 0.159] ($p{=}0.033$), with 12\\% leave-one-out instability (\\cref{app:multi_seed}). The CI excludes zero, confirming significance despite seed fragility. CP annotation perturbation (\\cref{tab:cp_perturbation}) further shows that $\\pm 2$-step label noise changes BA by ${<}2.5$pp, so annotation uncertainty does not compound with hyperparameter selection.\n\n\n\\section{Multi-Seed Stability}\n\\label{app:multi_seed}"

1	\section{Limitations}\label{sec:limitations}
2	
3	\paragraph{Model-specific caveats and statistical power.}
4	R1-32B achieves significance only at $W{=}1$ (25\% coverage, 5/20 traces) and its HS advantage is position-mediated (residualized gap +1.9pp, $p{=}0.914$; \cref{app:position_residual}). QwQ-32B's position baseline achieves higher balanced accuracy (0.832 vs.\ 0.796) with negative HS lead time. R1-8B FPR significance is seed-fragile (3/5 HB). At $n{=}19$--$20$, the minimum detectable effect at 80\% power is 8.6--9.5pp (\cref{app:power_analysis}); all 32B conclusions marked $\dagger$ should be interpreted with this constraint. Safe-trace FPR is 39--52\%, so current probes characterize the phenomenon rather than constitute production detectors; the 76.7\% oracle gap (\cref{sec:oracle}) confirms that passive probing captures only a fraction of available signal.
5	
6	\paragraph{Hyperparameter selection and scope.}
7	Layer (up to 64), $W$ (7 values), $K$ (5 values), and threshold are selected on the same validation set ($n{=}4$--$9$ per-class at 32B), yielding up to $2{,}240$ configurations; cumulative degrees of freedom are substantial. BA-optimal layer indices are sensitive to the train/validation split, spanning 7--98\% depth across models and splits (\cref{tab:ba_layer_comparison}); for example, OT-7B shifts from L16 to L2 and QwQ-32B from L60 to L63 between 70/30 and 60/20/20 splits, a direct manifestation of the layer selection sensitivity that constitutes our primary finding. Threshold-dependent metrics are more stable but still vary by up to 4 layers. $K$ was pre-selected (\cref{app:k_ablation}); joint (layer, $W$) stability was verified for QwQ-32B (\cref{app:layer_sweep}) but not R1-32B. CP annotations follow HarmThoughts~\citep{harmthoughts} without independent IAA evaluation; if annotation error is ${\geq}2$ steps, QwQ-32B's lead time ($-$1.2 to +1.9) falls within noise, though perturbation analysis shows smooth degradation with no cliff effects (\cr

{
  "text": "[写作进展] app:hyperparameter_robustness 完成\n\n## 新增内容\n\n### app:hyperparameter_robustness (appendix, position-residualized 之后)\n三段结构，直接回应 R7-W1 (cumulative hyperparameter overfitting):\n\n1. **Threshold-dependent metrics converge to deep-layer band** — 3/4 metrics 独立选择 L63 for both 32B; joint grid 192 configs 确认 near-optimal; R1-32B L55-L63 band 3/3 HB pass。搜索空间大但结果收敛，不符合过拟合特征。\n\n2. **BA-optimal layer instability is the finding** — 5-fold CV 表 (tab:fold_ba_layers): QwQ 和 R1-32B per-fold selections span 24 layers。这不是 limitation 而是核心发现。Mean-BA best (L50/L47) 与 LOO-CV dominant 一致。14/16 HB pass 证明优势不依赖特定 layer。\n\n3. **Bootstrap CIs quantify uncertainty** — R1-8B FPR BCa CI [0.006, 0.159], p=0.033, 12% LOO instability。CI 排除零，annotation perturbation 不叠加放大。\n\n### limitations.tex 级联\n§Hyperparameter 段加入引用: \"(\\cref{app:hyperparameter_robustness} provides convergence, cross-validation, and bootstrap analyses addressing this concern)\"\n\n## 编译状态\n0 critical warnings, 26 pages\n\n## Commit\n6859b4d\n\n## 状态\n等 Director 指示下一步。",

[Director 指令] ## 新增 Appendix Section: E5-Mistral LLM Encoder Comparison

### 内容

新增 `\section{LLM-Based Text Encoder Comparison}\label{app:e5_mistral}` 在 appendix 中 random projection section 之后。

**表格**（Agent 正在准备 LaTeX 格式，数据如下）:

| Model | Layer | W | HS Prec | E5 Prec | MiniLM Prec | ΔPrec(HS-E5) | p | HB |
|-------|-------|---|---------|---------|-------------|-------------|---|-----|
| R1-8B | L14 | 3 | .630 | .480 | .416 | +15.0pp | 0.001 | ✓ |
| OT-7B | L16 | 3 | .446 | .321 | .244 | +12.5pp | 0.009 | ✓ |
| R1-32B | L63 | 1 | .588 | .506 | .441 | +8.2pp | 0.235 | |
| QwQ-32B | L63 | 5 | .515 | .447 | .391 | +6.8pp | 0.368 | |

**Encoder gradient 汇总**（可嵌入同一 section 的叙事中）:
- MiniLM (22M, 384d): 4/4 HB
- E5-Mistral (7B, PCA 384d): 2/4 HB
- BGE-large (335M, 1024d): 1/4 HB

**叙事要点**（1-2 段，不超过半页）:
1. E5-Mistral-7B-Instruct（4096d→PCA 384d）作为 LLM-based encoder baseline
2. HS 在 2/4 模型（7-8B tier）保持 HB 显著优势
3. 三个 encoder 形成单调递减 gradient
4. **PCA caveat**: "PCA to 384d loses information; the comparison controls dimensionality but not representation quality"
5. 不要用 "robust against LLM encoders"，用 "partially survives"

### 正文交叉引用

在 `experiments.tex` §Capacity control 段末尾（约 L67，random projection 引用之后）加一句：

"An LLM-based encoder (E5-Mistral-7B, 4096d→PCA 384d) yields intermediate results (2/4 HB; \cref{app:e5_mistral}), forming a monotonic encoder-quality gradient with MiniLM (4/4) and BGE-large (1/4)."

### 注意
- MiniLM 数据来自 Table 3 (tab:hs_vs_text)
- BGE 数据来自 appendix encoder_robustness
- 不要用 Agent 汇报的 p 值，用 `artifacts/e5_mistral_comparison_v2/report.txt` 的数据

708	All four models retain significant HS advantage under random projection ($\Delta$Prec +8.3 to +20.5pp), with cross-seed standard deviations below 1.7pp. RP precision is slightly lower than PCA-384d (expected, as PCA preserves maximal variance), but the gap is small. This weakens a pure capacity explanation: even a structure-destroying projection preserves the HS advantage, suggesting the safety signal is broadly distributed across hidden-state dimensions rather than concentrated in top principal components.\n\n\n\\section{LLM-Based Text Encoder Comparison}\n\\label{app:e5_mistral}\n\nTo test whether an LLM-based text encoder closes the HS advantage gap, we encode each reasoning step with E5-Mistral-7B-Instruct~\\citep{wang2024e5mistral} (4096d, reduced to 384d via PCA to match the MiniLM dimensionality). \\cref{tab:e5_mistral} reports precision comparisons against HS probes at corrected layers.\n\n\\begin{table}[h]\n\\centering\n\\small\n\\caption{HS vs.\\ E5-Mistral-7B (PCA 384d) precision. HB: Holm-Bonferroni corrected (4 tests, $\\alpha{=}0.05$). MiniLM precision from \\cref{tab:hs_vs_text} for reference. 60/20/20 split.}\n\\label{tab:e5_mistral}\n\\begin{tabular}{llccccccc}\n\\toprule\n\\textbf{Model} & \\textbf{Layer} & $W$ & \\textbf{HS} & \\textbf{E5} & \\textbf{MiniLM} & $\\Delta$(HS$-$E5) & $p$ & \\textbf{HB} \\\\\n\\midrule\nR1-8B & L14 & 3 & .630 & .480 & .416 & +15.0 & 0.001 & \\checkmark \\\\\nOT-7B & L16 & 3 & .446 & .321 & .244 & +12.5 & 0.009 & \\checkmark \\\\\nR1-32B & L63 & 1 & .588 & .506 & .441 & +8.2 & 0.235 & \\\\\nQwQ-32B & L63 & 5 & .515 & .447 & .391 & +6.8 & 0.368 & \\\\\n\\bottomrule\n\\end{tabular}\n\\end{table}\n\nThe HS advantage partially survives against the LLM encoder: 2/4 models (both 7--8B) retain significance after Holm-Bonferroni correction. Three text encoders form a monotonic gradient: MiniLM (22M params, 384d): 4/4 HB; E5-Mistral (7B, PCA 384d): 2/4 HB; BGE-large (335M, 1024d): 1/4 HB (\\cref{app:encoder_robustness}). E5 precision consistently falls between MiniLM and HS across all models, confirming that encoder quality progressively narrows the gap. PCA to 384d discards information from the 4096d E5 representation; the comparison controls dimensionality but not representation quality, so the true E5 advantage may be larger than reported here.\n\n\n\\section{Position-Residualized Probing}\n\\label{app:position_residual}"
}

_source line 7422 · 2026-05-18T21:39:03.500983+00:00_

66	
67	\paragraph{Capacity control.} PCA reduction of HS features to 384d (matching text encoder dimensionality, 79--92\% variance retained) preserves the precision advantage across all four models$^\dagger$ (+16.8 to +23.1pp, all $p{<}0.012$; all model-level precision tests pass Holm-Bonferroni). Two individual metric comparisons are non-significant: R1-8B balanced accuracy (+1.3pp, $p{=}0.731$, effect too small at $n{=}43$) and QwQ-32B FPR ($-$11.4pp, $p{=}0.038$, below HB threshold). PCA retains directions of maximum variance in hidden-state space, which need not align with the semantic dimensions captured by a trained text encoder; the control provides partial evidence against a pure capacity explanation but does not rule out representation quality differences between PCA-compressed HS and trained text encoders. Random projection to 384d (structure-destroying, unlike PCA) yields similar results ($\Delta$Prec +8.3 to +20.5pp, all $p{<}0.001$; \cref{app:random_projection}), further weakening the capacity explanation.
68	
69	\paragraph{Encoder robustness.} The 1024d rows in \cref{tab:hs_vs_text} replace MiniLM (384d) with BGE-large~\citep{xiao2023cpack} (1024d, a 2.7$\times$ stronger encoder). The HS precision advantage persists directionally across all four models but narrows substantially: only OT-7B survives Holm-Bonferroni (1/4 precision tests). HS balanced accuracy advantage is also directionally positive across all four models (+8.4 to +10.9pp), with 2/4 (OT-7B, R1-32B) significant under Holm-Bonferroni; FPR advantage is significant only for OT-7B (1/4). R1-8B shows an adverse FPR direction (HS FPR 1.2pp higher than BGE, $p{=}0.564$). The advantage is encoder-conditional rather than absolute (\cref{fig:forest_precision}). Layer selection (Contribution 1) is encoder-independent, as it concerns hidden-state properties across layers rather than the text baseline.
70

This weakens a pure capacity explanation: even a structure-destroying projection preserves the HS advantage, suggesting the safety signal is broadly distributed across hidden-state dimensions rather than concentrated in top principal components.
 
 
+\section{LLM-Based Text Encoder Comparison}
+\label{app:e5_mistral}
+
+To test whether an LLM-based text encoder closes the HS advantage gap, we encode each reasoning step with E5-Mistral-7B-Instruct~\citep{wang2024e5mistral} (4096d, reduced to 384d via PCA to match the MiniLM dimensionality). \cref{tab:e5_mistral} reports precision comparisons against HS probes at corrected layers.

[Director 指令] ## 写作优化 — DDL 前最高 ROI（4 项，纯写作，不需新实验）

### 1. Discussion §Guidance 加 actionable takeaways（最高优先）

当前 discussion 最后段给了 "five-step audit" 建议，太 methodological。在该段之后（或融入其中）加 2-3 句面向 deployment 的具体指导：

- "For production monitoring: deep layers selected by threshold-dependent metrics with temporal windowing $W \geq 3$ provide the most reliable commitment detection."
- "For encoder choice: the crossover point where text encoders match HS probes lies approximately at the 1024d BGE-class level; below this, HS probes offer clear advantages."
- "For 32B models: HS probing is position-entangled; temporal windowing or explicit position features are necessary to extract non-positional signal."

目标：把论文从 pure characterization 升级为 "characterization with prescriptive recommendations"，直接回应 "so what?" 审稿人质疑。

### 2. Negative results 加深解释（§Discussion 或 §Experiments）

**BOCPD/CUSUM**：在正文 negative results 引用处加半句明确 sweep 范围。类似："BOCPD and CUSUM fail to detect commitment points reliably, even after sweeping hazard rate $\lambda \in \{5, 10, 20, 50, 100\}$ and drift $\delta \in \{0.5, 1.0, 2.0\}$ (\cref{app:bocpd_config})."

**Mahalanobis distance**：在 negative results 处加一句解释失败原因："Mahalanobis distance yields near-zero separation, consistent with safety signal being directional (linearly separable) rather than distributional (mode-separated) in hidden-state space."

### 3. Cross-transfer limitation

在 limitations 中加一句（§Method scope 相关位置）："Cross-model transfer was tested with PCA-based subspace alignment only; more sophisticated approaches (e.g., Procrustes rotation, CKA-guided projection) remain future work."

### 4. Oracle gap 措辞
已在 f033357 中修复（76.7% = early detection rate，非 lead time ratio）。请确认当前文本无残留问题。

### 写作约束
- 不增加正文超过 8 页（所有增量应在现有段落内融入）
- 不用 em-dash、-ing 分词结尾、AI 用词黑名单
- 保持 evidence gradient 叙事一致性

1	\section{Discussion}\label{sec:discussion}
2	
3	\paragraph{Layer selection as researcher degree of freedom.}
4	Our five-metric ablation (\cref{sec:layer-results}) shows that crossing rate reverses the central finding: at biased layers, the HS advantage appears scale-dependent; at corrected layers, it is significant across all four models$^\dagger$, from strong (OT-7B) to qualified (R1-32B$^\dagger$; \cref{sec:evaluation}). The mechanism is representation compression at shallow layers (\cref{app:cosine_volatility}), which inflates crossing rate independently of discriminative power. The effect likely generalizes to any threshold-based detection study. The HS advantage holds at both BA-optimal and precision-optimal layers regardless of depth: BA-optimal layers span 7--98\% depth across models and splits, yet 14/16 per-model tests pass HB (precision +12.8 to +18.6pp; \cref{tab:ba_layer_comparison}); the two FPR failures come from underpowered 32B test sets ($n{\leq}20$). That BA selection itself produces such depth variation reinforces the methodological finding. For R1-32B, layers L55--L63 at $W{=}1$ form a robust band where all 3/3 metrics pass HB (\cref{app:layer_sweep}).
5	
6	\paragraph{Encoder-quality boundary.}
7	The HS advantage is a function of text encoder quality: significant against 384d MiniLM across all four models$^\dagger$ (4/4 HB, strong to qualified) but attenuated against 1024d BGE-large (1/4 HB). PCA capacity matching preserves significance (+16.8 to +23.1pp), providing partial evidence against a pure capacity explanation. This boundary is itself a finding: the HS advantage represents information not yet captured by current sentence encoders rather than a fundamental modality difference. Temporal context and modality choice are complementary: at 7--8B, the modality advantage exceeds temporal improvement; temporal windowing ($W{=}3$ to $15$) provides consistent gains regardless of modality.
8

1	\section{Experiments}\label{sec:experiments}
2	
3	We evaluate temporal probes across four reasoning models (\cref{tab:models}), comparing hidden-state (HS) and text modalities under different layer selection criteria.
4	
5	\paragraph{Evidence gradient.} The strength of HS advantage evidence varies by model, not scale. We classify evidence into three tiers based on seed robustness, position independence, and sample size: \textbf{strong} (OT-7B: 5/5 seeds HB, $\Delta$Prec $+21.9$pp, no position confound, selectivity 11.1pp, $n{=}47$); \textbf{moderate} (R1-8B: precision-robust but FPR seed-fragile at 3/5 HB, $n{=}43$; QwQ-32B$^\dagger$: 5/5 HB, $\Delta$Prec $+19.2$pp, negative lead time, position-competitive BA, $n{=}19$); and \textbf{qualified} (R1-32B$^\dagger$: 5/5 HB, $\Delta$Prec std${=}2.0$pp, but position-mediated with residualized gap NS, $W{=}1$ only, $n{=}20$).
6	
7	\subsection{Setup}\label{sec:setup}
8	
9	We study R1-8B (Llama-8B, R1-distilled, $n{=}43$ test traces), OT-7B (OpenThinker-7B, trained on the OpenThoughts dataset~\citep{openthinker}; Qwen-7B backbone, STILL-trained, $n{=}47$), QwQ-32B (Qwen-32B, native reasoning, $n{=}19$--$20$), and R1-32B (Qwen-32B, R1-distilled, $n{=}20$). All traces come from HarmThoughts with step-level CP annotations (60/20/20 split). HS probes extract last-token hidden states from a selected layer; text probes encode each step with all-MiniLM-L6-v2 (384d). Both use L2-regularized logistic regression with temporal windowing $W \in \{1, 3, 5, 10, 15, 20, 25\}$ selected on validation. Detection requires $K{=}5$ consecutive threshold crossings. Statistical testing follows \cref{sec:evaluation}.
10

…[truncated 13029 chars]

1	\section{Limitations}\label{sec:limitations}
2	
3	\paragraph{Model-specific caveats and statistical power.}
4	R1-32B achieves significance only at $W{=}1$ (25\% coverage, 5/20 traces) and its HS advantage is position-mediated (residualized gap +1.9pp, $p{=}0.914$; \cref{app:position_residual}). QwQ-32B's position baseline achieves higher balanced accuracy (0.832 vs.\ 0.796) with negative HS lead time. R1-8B FPR significance is seed-fragile (3/5 HB). At $n{=}19$--$20$, the minimum detectable effect at 80\% power is 8.6--9.5pp (\cref{app:power_analysis}); all 32B conclusions marked $\dagger$ should be interpreted with this constraint. Safe-trace FPR is 39--52\%, so current probes characterize the phenomenon rather than constitute production detectors; the 76.7\% oracle gap (\cref{sec:oracle}) confirms that passive probing captures only a fraction of available signal.
5	
6	\paragraph{Hyperparameter selection and scope.}
7	Layer (up to 64), $W$ (7 values), $K$ (5 values), and threshold are selected on the same validation set ($n{=}4$--$9$ per-class at 32B), yielding up to $2{,}240$ configurations; cumulative degrees of freedom are substantial (\cref{app:hyperparameter_robustness} provides convergence, cross-validation, and bootstrap analyses addressing this concern). BA-optimal layer indices are sensitive to the train/validation split, spanning 7--98\% depth across models and splits (\cref{tab:ba_layer_comparison}); for example, OT-7B shifts from L16 to L2 and QwQ-32B from L60 to L63 between 70/30 and 60/20/20 splits, a direct manifestation of the layer selection sensitivity that constitutes our primary finding. Threshold-dependent metrics are more stable but still vary by up to 4 layers. $K$ was pre-selected (\cref{app:k_ablation}); joint (layer, $W$) stability was verified for QwQ-32B (\cref{app:layer_sweep}) but not R1-32B. CP annotations follow HarmThoughts~\citep{harmthoughts} without independent IAA evaluation; if annotation error is ${\geq}2$ steps, QwQ-32B's l

449	
450	\section{BOCPD/CUSUM Configuration Sweep}
451	\label{app:bocpd_config}
452	
453	We sweep hyperparameters for both Bayesian Online Change-Point Detection (BOCPD) and Cumulative Sum (CUSUM) methods applied to the temporal probe score sequences.
454	
455	\paragraph{BOCPD configurations.} BOCPD models the sequence of probe scores as piecewise-constant segments with Gaussian observation noise. The hazard rate $\lambda$ controls the prior probability of a change-point at each step ($P(\text{change}) = 1/\lambda$).
456	
457	\paragraph{CUSUM configurations.} CUSUM accumulates evidence for a positive mean shift. The drift parameter $\delta$ sets the minimum shift size to detect, and the threshold $h$ controls the detection boundary.
458	
459	\begin{table}[h]
460	\centering

467	Method & Configuration & Det.\ Rate & Lead \\

477	 & $\delta{=}0.3$, $h{=}3$ & 65.1 & +0.89 \\

614	
615	\paragraph{BOCPD/CUSUM.} On OT-7B, QwQ-32B, and R1-32B, BOCPD achieves 0\% detection rate across all tested configurations ($\lambda \in \{5, 10, 50, 100, 200\}$). Only R1-8B produces non-zero detection (best: $\lambda{=}10$, 93\% rate, +3.45 steps lead). CUSUM configurations universally underperform simple thresholding on lead time. The probe score sequences for 3/4 models show smooth transitions that never trigger the hazard function (\cref{app:bocpd_config}).
616	
617	\paragraph{Mahalanobis distance.} Following TrajGuard~\cite{trajguard}, Mahalanobis distance from the pre-CP centroid produces $\Delta d \approx 0$ between pre-CP and post-CP steps. The discriminative signal resides in minor PCA components (explaining $<$5\% of total variance) rather than the principal directions that dominate the distance computation.
618	
619	\paragraph{RepE.} Mean-difference directions~\cite{zou2023representation} show inconsistent performance: QwQ-32B achieves 0.756 balanced accuracy (approaching supervised 0.778) but with substantially worse precision (0.512 vs.\ LR 0.700) and FPR (0.309 vs.\ 0.120). R1-32B nearly fails (0.559 balanced accuracy). The mean-difference direction captures commitment structure in some models but not others, making it unreliable as a general-purpose method.
620	
621	\paragraph{MLP probes.} Two-layer MLPs ($d \rightarrow 128 \rightarrow 1$, ReLU) underperform logistic regression on 3/4 models. The commitment boundary is linearly separable at correctly selected layers; additional capacity causes overfitting given the small training sets.
622	
623	\paragraph{CCS.} Contrast-Consistent Search~\cite{burns2023discovering} adapted with contrastive pairs from pre-CP and post-CP steps provides no improvement over supervised probing. The logical consistency objective does not align with the temporal commitment structure. CCS still requires CP labels for pair construction, making it semi-supervised.
624	
625	\par

492	
493	The best BOCPD configuration ($\lambda{=}10$) achieves 93.0\% detection rate with +3.45 steps lead. The simple threshold baseline achieves 65.1\% detection with superior lead (+4.89 steps). All CUSUM configurations underperform the simple threshold on lead time.
494	
495	These results reveal a coverage-timing tradeoff. BOCPD ($\lambda{=}10$) achieves 28pp higher detection rate than simple thresholding (93.0\% vs.\ 65.1\%), but with lower lead (+3.45 vs.\ +4.89 steps). The transition toward unsafe reasoning is gradual rather than abrupt, so change-point methods cannot exploit discontinuities for early warning. When missed detections are costly, BOCPD's coverage advantage justifies its use despite the timing penalty. When early intervention is the priority, simple thresholding provides better lead time.

  "new_string": "We recommend a five-step audit for probing studies: (1)~train probes across all layers; (2)~compute $\\geq$3 metrics (threshold-free and threshold-dependent); (3)~check for metric disagreement; (4)~report 2nd/3rd best layers; (5)~compute cosine volatility (\\cref{app:cosine_volatility}). Steps 3--5 cost negligible compute. For production monitoring, deep layers selected by threshold-dependent metrics with temporal windowing $W \\geq 3$ provide the most reliable commitment detection. The crossover point where text encoders match HS probes lies approximately at the 1024d BGE-class level; below this, HS probes offer clear advantages. At 32B scale, HS probing is position-entangled, so temporal windowing or explicit position deconfounding is necessary to extract genuine safety signal (\\cref{app:position_residual})."
}

_source line 7482 · 2026-05-19T09:24:33.070218+00:00_

[Director 指令] [Patch] R16 审稿修复 — 全部写作/framing 变更，无新实验

**背景**: R16 Fresh Review 评分 3.0 (Findings)。目标是做稳 3.0，消除最大攻击面。DDL 5/25。这次不用验证引用。

论文源码: `/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0509_5/projects/contrastive_trace_safety/docs/paper/`

注意: `sections/experiments.tex` L73 已有一处未提交修改（FPR range 从 "39--52\%" 改为 "11--50\% false alarm rates (model-dependent)"），加上 `sections/discussion.tex` 有一处未提交的 contribution isolation sentence。请在此基础上继续修改。

---

## 修改清单（按优先级排序）

### P0: Critical fixes

**1. Abstract — 排除 R1-32B precision 数字 (W1)**
当前 Abstract 有 "+15.4 to +21.9pp, Holm-Bonferroni corrected" 涵盖 4 模型。
修改：改为 "+19.2 to +21.9pp precision advantage in three of four models (Holm-Bonferroni corrected); R1-32B's advantage is position-mediated (residualized gap below minimum detectable effect)"。
数据来源：R1-8B +20.1, QwQ +19.2, OT-7B +21.9。R1-32B +15.4 排除。

**2. Abstract — CP 定义更精确 (M5)**
CP 首次出现时加半句定义："the commitment point (CP), the reasoning step after which a model's chain of thought becomes irreversibly committed to an unsafe conclusion"。

**3. Abstract — scope 限定 (W4)**
加一句 scope："We study four reasoning LLMs on the HarmThoughts safety-probing benchmark."（或类似措辞，让读者知道是单数据集）

**4. K=5 justification (W3)**
Section 3（或 Appendix L）添加 K=5 理由：
- K=5 是 top-K layer selection 的常见设置
- Table 17 ablation 显示 K=3/5/7 下核心 HS vs text 结论方向一致（请查 Table 17 确认具体数字）
- 明确报告 K=3 和 K=7 下哪些模型的 HB 结论改变/不变
- 承认 K 是研究者自由度之一，但消融证据表明核心结论对 K 不敏感

**5. Discussion — 审计协议降格 (W5)**
当前 Discussion 有 "We propose a five-step audit"。改为 "Based on our findings, practitioners may consider a multi-metric audit workflow: ..." — 降格为 recommendation，不是 contribution。确认 Contributions 列表（Introduction 末尾）不包含审计协议（据 Reviewer 确认已经不包含）。

### P1: Major fixes

**6. 32B 分级证据体系 (W2)**
全文所有 "all four models†" 改为分级表述：
- 7-8B (R1-8B, OT-7B): "confirmed"
- QwQ-32B: "significant but position-competitive"
- R1-32B: "exploratory (below MDE)"

需要修改的位置：Abstract、Introduction (×2)、experiments.tex (×3+)、Discussion。每处根据上下文用自然措辞，不要机械替换。

Section 4.4 (32B Analysis) 开头加 "exploratory" 标签。报告有效 per-class 验证集大小（查 Table 1 数据）。加一句："Nested cross-validation was not feasible given the small per-class counts; LOO-CV and multi-seed analyses provide partial robustness checks but do not substitute for independent validation."

**7. Position mediation 更醒目 (W6)**
Section 4.3 position mediation 结果不应埋在 Caveats。在 Section 4.2 末尾或 Section 4.3 首段加 forward reference："Position features account for 9-20pp of balanced accuracy (Section 4.5); R1-32B's HS advantage is fully explained by position (residualized gap +1.9pp, p=0.914)."

**8. Related Work 扩充 (W7 + W8 + M3)**
"Probing Methodology" 段扩充：
- 引用 Tenney et al. (2019) 和 Jawahar et al. (2019)（已在 bib 中，需加 \cite{}）：层级信息分布是已知现象
- 关键区分：已知的语法/语义信息分布 vs 本文发现的安全探针 metric bias（crossing rate inflation at shallow layers）
- 与 Boxo et al. (2025) 直接对比 2-3 句：Boxo 发现 linear probes 依赖 textual evidence → 本文的 metric bias 分析提供互补机制解释（浅层探针利用 text-leakage 对应 Boxo 观察，本文进一步展示 metric 选择如何系统性导致选浅层）
- 考虑引用 Vig et al. (2020) causal mediation 到 Discussion position mediation 部分
- 删除 orphan bib entries: liu2019linguistic, meng2022rome, zhang2025probing_selfverify（先确认正文确实未引用）

**9. Introduction framing 调整 (W13)**
Introduction 开头从 "should read HS or text?" 二选一框架调整为方法论审计定位。不要完全删除实用动机（那是 hook），但尽早转向 "the reliability of HS safety probes depends on methodological choices — particularly layer selection — that have not been systematically examined"。

**10. BCa 小样本声明 (W10)**
Section 3.5（或统计方法段）加："For 32B models (n ≤ 20), the exact permutation test is the primary inference method; BCa confidence intervals are reported for comparability but may have reduced coverage at these sample sizes."

**11. LOO-CV 叙事统一 (W11)**
Section B (LOO-CV appendix) 和 Section V (instability)：统一为"32B 层选择不稳定 → 强化 Contribution 1"。Section B 删除 "robust" 一词，改为："LOO-CV shows moderate consistency for 7-8B models (65-68%) but lower for 32B, confirming that layer selection instability is particularly acute at small sample sizes — reinforcing the need for multi-metric auditing."

**12. 5 指标选择时序说明 (W12)**
Section 3 添加 1-2 句：(1) BA, Precision, FPR 是 probing 文献标配 metrics；crossing rate 和 composite 来自 safety detection 文献；(2) biased vs corrected 分类是观察到 metric disagreement 后的分析结论（诚实说明），但 Table 2 ablation 是可复现经验事实；(3) crossing rate inflation 的机制（浅层 cosine distance 压缩）是几何性质，有独立解释（引用 Appendix C cosine volatility 数据）。

**13. 模型多样性 Limitation (W9)**
Limitations 段加："Three of four models share the Qwen architecture; only R1-8B (Llama-based) provides cross-architecture evidence. Future work should include diverse base architectures."

### P2: Minor fixes

**14. Encoder-conditional 前向引用 (M1)**
Section 4.3 首段加一句："The HS advantage is encoder-conditional: against BGE-large (1024d), only OT-7B survives HB correction (Section 4.4; details in Appendix)."

**15. Selectivity benchmark (M2)**
Section H 或正文引用处加："Absolute selectivity values are task-dependent and not directly comparable across studies (Hewitt and Liang, 2019)."

**16. 表格小数精度 (M4)**
统一所有正文 Table 的 metric 精度。当前 Table 2 和 Table 3 不一致——统一为 1 位小数（pp 精度下 1 位足够）。

**17. GRU 数据划分 clarification (M6)**
确认 Appendix 已有 GRU dual split clarification note。如无则加一句。

**18. BOCPD 短链限制 (M7)**
Section 4.6 加半句："Change-point methods (BOCPD, CUSUM) are inherently limited on short reasoning chains; their failure here reflects trace-length constraints rather than a general inadequacy."

**19. Limitations — 单数据集升为首要 (W4 续)**
Limitations 第一段改为单数据集限制。具体讨论标注粒度/有害类型分布/标注团队差异可能影响层选择模式。

**20. Section 5 deployment (M8)**
确认已移至附录（commit 9dda6a2）。如仍在正文则移出。

---

## 写作硬约束
- 禁止 em-dash (—) 和解释性 en-dash (–)
- 禁止 -ing 短语结尾
- 正文不超过 8 页
- 修改后 `grep "—" sections/*.tex` 确认零 em-dash
- 每处修改要保持与上下文的逻辑衔接

## 交付
完成后 commit 并编译 PDF。报告修改摘要。

1	\section{Introduction}\label{sec:introduction}
2	
3	Reasoning models such as DeepSeek-R1~\cite{deepseekr1} and QwQ~\cite{qwq} scale inference-time computation through extended chains of thought (CoT). Recent work documents that these models can ``self-jailbreak'': without adversarial input modification, the CoT transitions from safety-aware deliberation to committed harmful generation~\cite{harmthoughts,selfjailbreak}. We define the first reasoning step after which safety deliberation permanently ceases as the \textbf{Safety Commitment Point} (CP). This failure mode differs qualitatively from adversarial jailbreaks on standard LLMs because the harmful transition originates within the model's own reasoning. Before building production detectors, a prior question must be answered: can current probing methods reliably distinguish pre-CP from post-CP reasoning steps, and under what conditions? This paper provides a systematic characterization of what hidden-state and text-based probes can and cannot detect.
4	
5	Existing safety detectors target fundamentally different problems. Hidden-state (HS) methods such as TrajGuard~\cite{trajguard} and SafeSwitch~\cite{safeswitch} classify adversarial inputs at the token level, while text monitors like SafeRemind~\cite{saferemind} scan generated reasoning for explicit violations. Neither tracks the gradual, multi-step commitment process. A fundamental question remains: should a monitoring system read the model's hidden states or its generated text? This comparison has never been conducted under controlled conditions, and a further complication is that HS probes require selecting which transformer layer to monitor. Layer selection is a researcher degree of freedom whose metric sensitivity has received almost no scrutiny, yet we show it can reverse a study's central conclusion.
6	
7	We present the first systematic evaluation across four reasoning models spanning two architectures (Llama, Qwen) and two scales (7--8B, 32B). Our centr
…[truncated 2370 chars]

1	\section{Problem Setup and Methods}
2	\label{sec:method}
3	
4	\subsection{Safety Commitment Point}
5	\label{sec:commitment-point}
6	
7	When a reasoning model is jailbroken, its chain-of-thought (CoT) transitions from safety-aware deliberation to committed harmful generation. We define the \textbf{Safety Commitment Point} (CP) as the first reasoning step after which safety reasoning permanently ceases. Steps before CP are \emph{pre-commitment} ($y_t = 0$); steps at or after CP are \emph{post-commitment} ($y_t = 1$). The detection task produces a score $s_t \in [0, 1]$ estimating whether the model has passed its CP by step $t$.
8	
9	We study four reasoning models spanning two architectures and two scales (\cref{tab:models}).

19	OT-7B & Qwen & 7B & 28 & 3584 & 228\,/\,47 \\
20	R1-32B & Qwen & 32B & 64 & 5120 & 100\,/\,20 \\
21	QwQ-32B & Qwen & 32B & 64 & 5120 & 95\,/\,19 \\
22	\bottomrule
23	\end{tabular}}

Data: HarmThoughts~\citep{harmthoughts} with step-level CP annotations. Split: 60/20/20.}
25	\label{tab:models}
26	\end{table}

1	\section{Discussion}\label{sec:discussion}
2	
3	\paragraph{Layer selection as researcher degree of freedom.}
4	Our five-metric ablation (\cref{sec:layer-results}) shows that crossing rate reverses the central finding: at biased layers, the HS advantage appears scale-dependent; at corrected layers, it is significant across all four models$^\dagger$. The mechanism is shallow-layer representation compression (\cref{app:cosine_volatility}), likely generalizable to any threshold-based detection study. The finding is robust: BA-optimal layers span 7--98\% depth, yet 14/16 per-model tests pass HB (\cref{tab:ba_layer_comparison}); three independent threshold-dependent metrics converge to L63 for both 32B models despite small validation sets ($n{=}10$--$20$); and R1-32B layers L55--L63 form a robust band at $W{=}1$ (\cref{app:layer_sweep}). This robustness separates the two contributions: layer selection sensitivity (Contribution~1) follows from representation geometry and holds regardless of sample size, while the empirical HS advantage (Contribution~2) is sample-dependent and requires larger-scale validation.
5	
6	\paragraph{Encoder-quality boundary.}
7	The HS advantage is significant against 384d MiniLM (4/4 HB$^\dagger$) but attenuated against 1024d BGE-large (1/4 HB). PCA capacity matching preserves significance (+16.8 to +23.1pp), providing partial evidence against a pure capacity explanation. This boundary is itself a finding: the HS advantage represents information not yet captured by current sentence encoders rather than a fundamental modality difference. As encoders improve, this boundary will shift.
8	
9	\paragraph{Guidance for future research.}
10	Oracle analysis corroborates the evidence gradient (\cref{sec:oracle}): OT-7B achieves 93.6\% early detection vs.\ R1-32B at 50\%, consistent with distillation and native reasoning producing different safety representation geometry. Converging evidence (PCA capacity control, control task selectivity, position residual
